- **thin-layer-degeneracy**: Judge how much a thin layer in a reflectometry model can actually be trusted, and why a chi-squared or BIC comparison can reject a layer that is really there. - **thingworx-extension-metadata-validator**: Use whenever writing, editing, or reviewing a ThingWorx extension's metadata.xml — before packaging, before import, or whenever asked to check/validate extension metadata. This is the single hardest-to-diagnose ThingWorx extension failure observed; this skill catches it statically. ([FluffyProgramming/thingworx-mcp-building-block](https://github.com/FluffyProgramming/thingworx-mcp-building-block/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/thingworx-extension-metadata-validator))

- **thingworx-import-readiness**: Use whenever writing, editing, or reviewing a bundle of ThingWorx entity XML exports (Things, ThingTemplates, ThingShapes, DataShapes, Resources) — before importing into Composer, before committing a multi-entity export, or whenever asked to check/validate cross-entity consistency. Entities can fail to import, or import and silently no-op, because of dangling references (a thingTemplate/thingShape/dataShape that doesn't exist anywhere), duplicate entity names, or a service building InfoTable rows with field names that don't match its declared DataShape. This skill builds a model of everything… ([FluffyProgramming/thingworx-mcp-building-block](https://github.com/FluffyProgramming/thingworx-mcp-building-block/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/thingworx-import-readiness))
- **thingworx-layer-propagation-check**: Use whenever adding or renaming a field/feature that has to be wired through multiple ThingWorx layers at once (DataShape, stored procedure, wrapper service, print template, translation, ...) — before committing, or whenever asked to check/validate that a change propagated everywhere it needs to. A single missed layer breaks the feature end to end even when every other layer is correct, and there's no compiler to catch it. This skill checks a small JSON manifest describing which layers a field must appear in against the actual files, and reports which layers are missing it. ([FluffyProgramming/thingworx-mcp-building-block](https://github.com/FluffyProgramming/thingworx-mcp-building-block/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/thingworx-layer-propagation-check))
- **thingworx-localization-parity**: Use whenever writing, editing, or reviewing translation files for a ThingWorx project — .po files per language (en/fr/ja or your configured set), or the Default.xml localization table — before committing, or whenever asked to check/validate translation-key parity. Translations maintained in parallel across multiple .po files and a Default.xml localization table commonly drift: an edit updates one language or one side and not the other, and nothing catches it until a user sees an untranslated or missing string. This skill checks key parity across all of them statically. ([FluffyProgramming/thingworx-mcp-building-block](https://github.com/FluffyProgramming/thingworx-mcp-building-block/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/thingworx-localization-parity))
- **thingworx-mssql-dialect-lint**: Use whenever writing, editing, or reviewing SQL meant to run on Microsoft SQL Server — a .sql file, or SQL service/stored-procedure code inside a ThingWorx entity XML export's CDATA block — before committing, before deploying a proc, or whenever asked to check/lint/validate SQL for MSSQL/T-SQL compatibility. Non-SQL-Server syntax (MySQL/ANSI dialect — LIMIT, backtick identifiers, AUTO_INCREMENT, IFNULL, NOW(), LENGTH(, SUBSTR(, GROUP_CONCAT, double-quoted string literals) won't parse on MSSQL at all, or silently means something different. This skill catches that statically. ([FluffyProgramming/thingworx-mcp-building-block](https://github.com/FluffyProgramming/thingworx-mcp-building-block/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/thingworx-mssql-dialect-lint))
- **thingworx-rhino-lint**: Use whenever writing, editing, or reviewing a ThingWorx service's JavaScript (a .js file, or service code inside a ThingWorx entity XML export's CDATA block) — before committing, before import, or whenever asked to check/lint/validate ThingWorx service code for Rhino compatibility. - **thingworx-spec-open-items-gate**: Use whenever writing, reviewing, or marking complete a spec markdown file (a superpowers-style spec, or any project spec) — before committing it, or whenever asked to check/validate/gate a spec for open items. Use before transfers, bulk file work, looped remote or API calls, and whenever a step is much slower than expected. Explores user intent, requirements and design before implementation. ([ehadziabdic/WAgents](https://github.com/ehadziabdic/WAgents/tree/HEAD/opencode/skills/think-brainstorm))
- **think-brainstorm**: Good-faith divergent brainstorming for achieving a goal. Validates assumptions, then spawns brainstormers running different techniques (first-principles, working-backwards, lateral, analogical, constraints-shift, etc.) in isolation, and synthesizes the pool into a report of standouts, reasonable ideas, and hybrid combinations. Produces feedback only — no code, no tickets, no artifacts. ([shivamchopra7/claude-skill-directory](https://github.com/shivamchopra7/claude-skill-directory/tree/HEAD/skills/analysis/think-brainstorm) · 2 copies)
- **think-brainwriting**: Generates ideas the way silent parallel brainwriting does, producing several independent idea streams that build on each other without anchoring on the first voice, then consolidates them into a shortlisted idea pool. Use when you need breadth of options and want to avoid the anchoring and conformity that make ordinary brainstorming underperform. ([product-on-purpose/thinking-framework-skills](https://github.com/product-on-purpose/thinking-framework-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/think-brainwriting) · ★26)
- **think-canvas**: Work on a think canvas (the brainstorming app's boards) — start one, add or rearrange blocks, frames and links, answer comments, report back in its chat — with the `think` command. ([olalha/think-board](https://github.com/olalha/think-board/tree/HEAD/skills/think-canvas) · ★7)
- **think-carefully**: 不管回答什么问题，都要仔细思考。在回答任何问题或执行任何任务之前，先深入分析和推理。 ([binjie09/zknow-skills](https://github.com/binjie09/zknow-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/think-carefully))
- **think-causal**: Causal reasoning methods — Causal analysis (cause-effect mechanisms, causal graphs, confounders) and Counterfactual reasoning (what-if scenarios, alternative histories, intervention analysis). Use when the user invokes `/think causal` or `/think counterfactual`, or asks about cause-and-effect relationships, confounders, or "what would have happened if..." questions. ([danielsimonjr/deepthinking-mcp](https://github.com/danielsimonjr/deepthinking-mcp/tree/HEAD/skills/think-causal) · ★4)
- **think-causal-layered-analysis**: Produces a four-layer matrix (litany, system, worldview, myth) of the current 'used future' beside a reconstructed preferred future per layer, anchored by a deliberately changed deep metaphor. Use when an issue is stuck because the framing is stuck, the official account and the system explanation have been argued to exhaustion, and the real disagreement is about clashing worldviews and the unexamined story underneath. Not a single-cause diagnosis (that is think-iceberg-model) and not a forecast. ([product-on-purpose/thinking-framework-skills](https://github.com/product-on-purpose/thinking-framework-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/think-causal-layered-analysis) · ★26)
- **think-causal-loop-diagrams**: Builds a signed causal loop diagram by closing the feedback loops in a situation, labeling each loop reinforcing (R) or balancing (B) with its link polarities, and reading likely dynamics (spiral, goal-seeking, or oscillation) off which loop dominates. Use when a situation feeds back on itself - growth that funds more growth, a fix that recreates its own problem, capacity that relieves then re-attracts demand - and you need to see why it keeps accelerating, stalling, or overshooting. Not for a single accumulation, a one-directional consequence tree, or a genuinely linear chain. ([product-on-purpose/thinking-framework-skills](https://github.com/product-on-purpose/thinking-framework-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/think-causal-loop-diagrams) · ★26)
- **think-cell-charts**: Build evidence-backed management PowerPoint slides from Excel/CSV or other structured business data using an enforced analysis → storyline → presentation-template → official think-cell ppttc/JSON workflow. Use for performance calibration, saving bridges, HC mix, labor productivity, regional benchmarks, waterfall, stacked/clustered column or bar, line, Mekko, and Gantt/timeline requests where charts must remain genuinely editable with think-cell and must not be replaced by native PowerPoint charts. ([Sidliu22/think-cell-agent-skill](https://github.com/Sidliu22/think-cell-agent-skill/blob/HEAD/SKILL.md))

Use when Codex must turn ambiguous project, feature, workflow, webpage, document, or skill requests into a controllable planning handoff while reducing user burden without weakening critical confirmation. ([danhnbui/prototype-builder](https://github.com/danhnbui/prototype-builder/tree/HEAD/pb/skills/think-clarify) · ★2)
- **think-closed-loop**: 思维模型：闭环。一件事要从一方转到另一方之前，先过三句：转手会丢信息，做对它要用到的写不下来的上下文多就不转；回路越紧越稳，决定后面很多步的事放在每一步都看得见的回路里；一块多大由接的一方验得完多少定。准备派子代理、把一件事交给人或另一个会话、定一程或一片多大、上下文快被压缩时加载；人说「这件事你自己做」「一块别太大」「多久看一次」，或点名 /think-closed-loop 时也加载。自己一口气做完、不转手的小活不加载。派发简报的写法与花费归 delegate-brief。 ([PanQiWei/pqw-skills](https://github.com/PanQiWei/pqw-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/think-closed-loop) · ★1)
- **think-cog**: Complex ideas need iteration, not conversation. Think → Do → Review → Repeat. - **think-concept-mapping**: Builds a concept map - a non-hierarchical network of concept nodes joined by directed, labeled linking phrases so each node-link-node reads as an explicit proposition, with cross-links across clusters - then surfaces gaps, missing links, and questionable propositions. Use when a domain has many interrelated concepts and the goal is to externalize and inspect how they relate, forcing every relationship to be named rather than left as a vague association. - **think-consider-the-unknowns**: Produces a known-unknowns ledger by enumerating the relevant evidence not in hand, the variables that bear on a judgment but are unknown or unobservable, rating each unknown by its bearing and its obtainability, then re-rating confidence against the mapped gap. Use when a consequential one-off judgment rests on thin or partial evidence, no usable base rate exists, and the confidence on the table may be inflated by what was never looked at. - **think-contradiction-resolution**: Converts a stuck trade-off into a contradiction to dissolve rather than a compromise to settle for. Use when an organization keeps oscillating destructively between two opposites that are both true and interdependent (centralize versus decentralize, stability versus change, candor versus diplomacy), and treating each swing as a problem to solve is the actual mistake. Not for a dissolvable trade-off and not for a real either/or choice.
